# AGENTS Guidelines
- Act as a world-class Senior Web Developer with extensive full-stack experience. Your goal is to construct an exceptional, - production-ready website that exceeds my original requirements.
- Write clean, robust, and completely mistake-free code.
- Elevate my specifications by applying top-tier UI/UX, web security, performance optimization, and accessibility best practices.
- Proactively identify edge cases, refine architecture, and deliver optimal, highly scalable solutions."

```
+-------------------------------------------------------+
|  [ Search (/) ] - Page 1 - Page 2 - Page 3  [ Logo ]  |
|        Home   About   Services   Contact   [CTA]      |
|_______________________________________________________|
|                         HEADER                        |
+-------------------------------------------------------+
|                     HERO SECTION                      |
|          Main Headline & Value Proposition            |
|                   [ Action Button ]                   |
+----------------------------------+--------------------+
|           MAIN CONTENT           |      SIDEBAR       |
|  - Primary Content / Features    |  - Search Bar      |
|  - Product Cards / Images        |  - Quick Links     |
+----------------------------------+--------------------+
|                        FOOTER                         |
| Privacy Policy | Terms of Service | Social | © 2026   |
+-------------------------------------------------------+
```

## 1. USER EXPERIENCE

### Framework for Elevating Organizational User Experience (UX)

Elevating User Experience (UX) is not merely about creating polished interface designs; it requires building a systematic capability that delivers consistent user value and measurable business returns. Real UX maturity is achieved when user-centered research, design, and strategy are embedded into the culture, processes, and high-level decision-making of an organization.

---

### 1. Diagnose and Map UX Maturity

To advance UX within an organization, you must first evaluate your current maturity level across four core factors:
*   **Strategy:** How UX leadership, planning, and resource prioritization are structured.
*   **Culture:** The widespread awareness, appreciation, and career growth of UX across non-UX teams.
*   **Process:** The systematic use of qualitative/quantitative research, iterative design, and shared frameworks.
*   **Outcomes:** The intentional definition and measurement of design impact on business metrics.

#### The 6 Stages of UX Maturity
Organizations generally evolve through six distinct stages:
1.  **Absent:** UX is ignored or non-existent.
2.  **Limited:** UX activities are rare, sporadic, or driven purely by legal compliance or individual experimental efforts.
3.  **Emergent:** UX work is functional and promising across multiple teams, but remains inconsistent and vulnerable to budget trade-offs.
4.  **Structured:** The organization features an established UX team and a semi-systematic, iterative design methodology, though strategic gaps remain.
5.  **Integrated:** UX work is pervasive, highly effective, and directly ties into leadership's core success metrics.
6.  **User-Driven:** Dedication to user-centered design shapes overall business strategy, driving innovation and market investment.

> **Key Rule:** Progress must occur incrementally. Attempting to leapfrog stages (e.g., jumping from Stage 2 to Stage 4) is unrealistic. Sustainable growth requires moving step-by-step (\\(N \rightarrow N+1\\)) as user-centered practices permeate the organization.

---

### 2. Formulate a Practical UX Strategy

A robust UX strategy prevents design teams from becoming purely tactical "wireframe assembly lines" for external requests. Structure your strategy into three core phases:

```
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                1. DIAGNOSIS                                     |
|  - Identify Status Quo Failures   - Audit Resources, Skills & Influence         |
|  - Map Aspirational User Journeys - Define Technical & Operational Constraints  |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                        │
                                        ▼
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                            2. GUIDING POLICIES                                  |
|  - Choose Operating Model         - Establish Digital Triage Prioritization     |
|  - Build a UX Playbook            - Implement RACI & Governance Frameworks      |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                        │
                                        ▼
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                               3. ACTION PLAN                                    |
|  - Scope High-Impact Sprints      - Deploy Shared Research & Tooling            |
|  - Secure Executive Sponsors      - Establish Fallbacks for Risk Mitigation     |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

#### A. Diagnosis: Establish Your Baseline
*   **Identify Status Quo Failures:** Frame current design weaknesses in concrete business terms (e.g., a complex sign-up flow losing 20% of conversions monthly).
*   **Map Aspirational Experiences:** Compare current customer journey maps against ideal, streamlined paths across discovery, onboarding, and support phases.
*   **Audit Influence & Constraints:** Map out key allies (product management, marketing) alongside hard technical debt, headcount freezes, or platform limitations.

#### B. Guiding Policies: Set the Operating Rules
*   **Choose an Operational Model:** 
    *   *Tactical Model:* Embed UX practitioners directly into project squads (aiming for roughly a 1:2 UX-to-developer ratio) for hands-on execution.
    *   *Strategic Model:* Function as a **Center of Excellence**, empowering other departments via guidelines, tooling, and workshops.
*   **Establish Digital Triage:** Sort incoming requests using a transparent scoring formula based on **Impact**, **Effort**, and **Risk**.
*   **Construct a Playbook of Principles:** Maintain a centralized repository of core UI patterns, research standards, accessibility guidelines, and content templates.
*   **Plan Communication:** Deploy a **RACI chart** (Responsible, Accountable, Consulted, Informed) to structure cross-departmental engagement.

#### C. Action Plan: Execute and Mitigate Risk
*   Deliver value in small, sprint-ready project increments to build organizational credibility quickly.
*   Assign clear executive roles to champion budget line items and break through interdepartmental roadblocks.
*   Define fallbacks for potential risks (such as pivot plans if hiring freezes delay tactical recruits).

---

### 3. Align UX Metrics with Business Value & Secure Buy-In

To persuade leadership, design initiatives must be framed not as cosmetic polish, but as business-centered enablers of revenue, retention, and risk mitigation.

```
                     [Business Objective]
                      (e.g., Increase Revenue)
                                │
                  ┌─────────────┴─────────────┐
                  ▼                           ▼
          [Key Business Metric]      [Key Business Metric]
         (e.g., Reduce Churn)      (e.g., Higher Conversion)
                  │                           │
          ┌───────┴───────┐           ┌───────┴───────┐
          ▼               ▼           ▼               ▼
      [UX Driver]     [UX Driver] [UX Driver]     [UX Driver]
       (Task Speed)  (Error Rate) (Usability)  (Onboarding)
```

#### Build a Design KPI Tree
Map high-level business goals down to granular UX metrics:
1.  **Business Goal:** Increase customer lifetime value / reduce operational expenses.
2.  **Product Metric:** Reduce support ticket volume or checkout drop-off rate.
3.  **Design KPI:** Task completion time, misclick rate, error recovery rate, or System Usability Score.

#### Deliver Persuasive Stakeholder Presentations
*   **Use the Pyramid Principle:** Lead directly with the key finding and its business implication before detailing methodology (e.g., *"Simplifying navigation will reduce user drop-off by 15%, saving \$200k in lost registrations"*).
*   **Leverage Visual & Direct Evidence:** Combine quantitative usability metrics (task completion rates, heatmaps) with qualitative clips of users struggling.
*   **Preemptively Address Objections:**
    *   *Objection:* "Research takes too long." \\(\rightarrow\\) *Reframe:* Lean 5-user usability tests identify 80% of critical issues in days, preventing multi-sprint engineering re-works.
    *   *Objection:* "UX is too expensive." \\(\rightarrow\\) *Reframe:* Upfront usability research is \\(10\times\\) to \\(100\times\\) cheaper than fixing errors post-release.

---

### 4. Cultivate Culture and Operational Excellence

High UX maturity requires embedding user empathy across all teams, not just within the design department.

```
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                        OPERATIONAL EXCELLENCE PILLARS                             |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  1. Exposure Hours      │ 2. Dev-Design Overlaps  │ 3. Quality Buffer             |
|  Minimum 2 hours per    │ Weekly 20-min joint   │ Reserve 20% of team           |
|  6 weeks for every team │ "show-and-tell" to link │ sprint capacity for technical |
|  member to observe users│ product & engineering.  │ & design debt reduction.      |
|                   │                   │                         |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

1.  **Establish Exposure Hours:** Set a target for every team member (including developers and managers) to observe live user research for at least **2 hours every 6 weeks**.
2.  **Bridge Design and Engineering:** Run weekly 20-minute joint "show-and-tell" sessions where designers and developers share work-in-progress, resolve edge cases, and align on technical feasibility.
3.  **Reserve 20% Capacity for Quality & Design Debt:** Allocate 80% of sprint capacity to new feature delivery and 20% to resolving design debt, polishing micro-interactions, and refining system architecture.
4.  **Make UX Tangible:** Display customer journey maps, key personas, and usability metrics in shared physical or digital workspaces.

---

### 5. Execute High-Fidelity Design Audits

A mature UX strategy requires rigorous quality checks during interface execution. Interfaces often fail when teams design only for the "happy path" or accept generic AI/template defaults.

#### A. Perform Critical Flow Audits
Select one critical business flow (e.g., checkout or onboarding) and audit it against four core criteria:
*   **Primary Action Clarity:** Is the most important next step clear, or is it buried under visual clutter?
*   **Product-Specific Authenticity:** Could a competitor copy-paste your interface and content word-for-word? If so, replace generic copy and stock assets with specific, real-world product workflows and context.
*   **Feedback & System Responsiveness:** Is visual feedback immediate upon click or input?
*   **Technical Foundations:** Is performance fast under spotty network connections? Is Information Architecture (IA) structured logically?

#### B. Design for Uncomfortable & Edge-Case States
Ensure engineering specs and design components systematically address all non-standard UI states:

| UI State | Mandatory Design Behavior |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Empty State** | Provide clear guidance, contextual onboarding, or initial actions rather than a blank screen. |
| **Loading State** | Implement skeleton screens or indicators to manage perceived latency. |
| **Partial Failure** | Display explicit error messaging while keeping unimpacted system controls functional. |
| **Validation Error** | Provide immediate, line-item feedback explaining *how* to correct the error. |
| **Permission Denied** | Clearly explain access restrictions and offer a path forward. |

#### C. Guarantee Technical and Accessibility Standards
Ensure all production interfaces comply with international standards (such as WCAG 2.2 AA):
*   Maintain a minimum text contrast ratio of **4.5:1** (3:1 for large text) and non-text UI element contrast of **3:1**.
*   Provide visible focus indicators and ensure full keyboard navigation support.
*   Ensure interactive tap targets are at least **24x24 pixels**.
*   Ensure layouts adapt dynamically without breaking at 320px widths or 200% zoom levels.

## 2. USER INTERFACE

### Framework for Elevating User Interface (UI) Execution & Design

Elevating User Interface (UI) design requires moving beyond statistical averages, generic template defaults, and superficial polish to construct intentional, branded, and highly usable interfaces. True UI excellence occurs when layout structure, typography, color systems, and interface states are systematically aligned with brand identity and technical accessibility standards.

---

### 1. Diagnose and Eliminate "AI Design Slop"

Generative models suffer from **distributional convergence**—predicting tokens based on high-probability statistical averages, which produces repetitive, interchangeable interface defaults. Elevating UI starts with auditing and stripping out these predictable tropes:

```
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                           COMMON UI SLOP PATTERNS TO REMOVE                       |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  1. Color Tropes        │ Diagonal purple-to-blue gradients; gradient text |
|                         │ headlines; low-contrast dark mode (slate-900) |
|                         │; single-color register palettes. |
|-------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------|
|  2. Typography Tropes   │ Single default font across all weights (Inter/Roboto)   |
|                         │; single italic serif accent words; |
|                         │ emojis in headings; excessive em dashes.|
|-------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------|
|  3. Component Tropes    │ Rigid 3-card equal grids; 4px left-border card |
|                         │ stripes; frosted-glass backdrop blurs;|
|                         │ floating fake dashboards; pill badges above heads.|
|-------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------|
|  4. Motion & Finish     │ Global grain/noise overlays; cursor light glows|
|                         │; scroll fade-in animations on all sections.|
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*   **Color & Gradients:** Eliminate diagonal indigo-to-violet background gradients, clipped multi-color gradient headline text that degrades legibility outdoors, low-contrast mid-grey body text on dark backgrounds, and monochromatic single-color registers where icons, borders, and cards blend together.
*   **Typography & Copy Displays:** Stop using a single neutral sans-serif font across every weight without visual contrast. Remove single italic serif words inserted into headlines to signal fake "craft", emojis used as functional bullet icons, and long em dashes bridging fragmented thoughts.
*   **Component Structure:** Abandon rigid 3-column feature card layouts, 4px colored left-border accent stripes on cards, semi-transparent frosted-glass containers, floating fake analytics dashboards hovering at an angle, pill-shaped badges reading "AI-Powered" above headlines, decorative eyebrow labels, and uniform thin outline icons.
*   **Finish & Interactivity:** Strip away global grain noise overlays, cursor-tracking light glows, indiscriminate scroll fade-in animations that cause blank screens on slow connections, and uniform rounded corners applied to every visual container.

---

### 2. Formulate Pre-Design Strategy & System Boundaries

High-quality UI is downstream of strict creative constraints. Establishing visual boundaries prior to prompting AI tools or writing code prevents models from defaulting to generic layout averages.

```
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                           1. PRE-DESIGN PREPARATION                             |
|  - Hand-Sketch Layouts Before Coding   - Collect 10-15 Visual References        |
|  - Define 4-Part Briefing Foundation   - Reject "Modern and Clean" Prompts      |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                        │
                                        ▼
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                       2. NAMED AESTHETIC DIRECTIONS                             |
|  - Editorial (Serif type, asymmetric)  - Technical (Monospace, structural grid) |
|  - Minimal (High space, 1 accent)      - Brutalist (Hard edges, sharp corners)  |
|  - Warm (Off-white grounds, earth)                                              |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                        │
                                        ▼
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                       3. DESIGN SYSTEM & ASSET RULES                            |
|  - Enforce Mathematical Spacing Scale  - Restrain Color to 1 Brand Accent       |
|  - Source Real Product Screenshots     - Replace Stock Humans with Real Team    |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

#### A. Pre-Design Preparation
1.  **Hand-Sketch Layouts First:** Draw section flows and component arrangements on paper before touching code or AI builders. Sketching forces deliberate decisions regarding visual hierarchy and prevents AI from injecting default section orders.
2.  **Curate Mood Boards:** Collect 10–15 visual references from Behance, Dribbble, or Pinterest to calibrate your eye and feed explicit aesthetic guidelines into design briefs.
3.  **Enforce a 4-Part Brief:** Establish a clear foundation upfront: **(1) Brand Name, (2) Specific Offer, (3) Target Audience, and (4) Primary Page Goal**. Leaving gaps forces the tool to fill them with statistical fluff.

#### B. Commit to a Named Aesthetic Direction
Reject vague descriptors like "modern, clean, and elegant". Prompt and build within a defined aesthetic framework:
*   **Editorial:** High-contrast serif headlines, generous measure, asymmetric grids, and restrained typography.
*   **Technical:** Monospace type accents, high information density, visible structural borders instead of drop shadows, and stark functional contrast.
*   **Minimal:** Ample whitespace, high typographic scale contrast, single accent color, and absolute visual restraint.
*   **Brutalist:** Hard edges, zero corner radii, heavy display type, and high-contrast monochrome tones.
*   **Warm:** Off-white or cream grounds, muted earth tones, and approachable display type.

---

### 3. Core Visual & Layout System Refinement

```
GENERIC AI LAYOUT (Avoid)                    HIGH-FIDELITY UI LAYOUT (Target)
+------------------------------------+       +------------------------------------+
|   [Badge: AI-Powered ✨]           |       | BRAND NAME          Services  About|
|   Gradient Headline Text (Purple)  |       |                                    |
|   [Centered CTA Button]            |       | Distinctive Display Headline       |
|   Floating Fake 3D Dashboard       |       | Solid Ink | Asymmetric Layout      |
+------------------------------------+       | [Primary Action Button]            |
| [Icon] [Icon] [Icon]               |       +------------------------------------+
| 3 Equal Cards w/ Left Borders      |       | REAL PRODUCT SCREENSHOT / PHOTO    |
+------------------------------------+       +-------------------+----------------+
| Scroll Fade-in | Noise Grain Overlay|       | Main Feature Card | Detail Panel   |
+------------------------------------+       | (Variable Scale)  | (High Density) |
                                             +-------------------+----------------+
```

#### A. Typography & Color Precision
*   **Solid Ink & Hierarchy:** Set main headlines in solid ink rather than clipped gradients to protect legibility. Combine a distinctive display face for headings with a clean body font, creating clear weight and size gaps to establish hierarchy.
*   **Restrained Palette:** Select one primary brand color derived from physical assets, logos, or real product environments. Reserve high visual contrast exclusively for primary interactive controls and critical status changes.

#### B. Structural Layout & Spacing Scale
*   **Mathematical Spacing Scale:** Enforce a strict spacing scale (e.g., **4, 8, 16, 24, 32, 48, 64 pixels**) across all containers, margins, and padding to eliminate layout drift.
*   **Vary Section & Container Geometry:** Break rigid 3-card mathematical symmetry. Vary card dimensions based on content importance—letting critical features occupy larger layout blocks while supporting details remain compact.
*   **Authentic Photography:** Replace synthetic stock humans and glowing abstract 3D loops with high-resolution screenshots of actual software UI, real product photography, and verified team members.

---

### 4. Edge-Case UI States & Accessibility Standards

Polished interfaces often break when moving off the "happy path". A resilient UI systematically handles all interaction edge cases and meets accessibility requirements.

#### A. Design for Non-Happy-Path UI States

| UI State | Mandatory Design Behavior |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Empty State** | Provide clear guidance, contextual onboarding, or initial call-to-action buttons rather than an empty blank container. |
| **Loading State** | Implement structured skeleton screens matching content shapes rather than vague spinners. |
| **Partial Failure** | Display explicit error messaging while keeping unimpacted system panels fully interactive. |
| **Validation Error** | Provide immediate, line-item feedback directly adjacent to the input field explaining *how* to correct the entry. |
| **Permission Denied** | Clearly state access boundaries and present an explicit request or upgrade path. |

#### B. Guarantee Accessibility Standards (WCAG 2.2 AA)
*   **Text & UI Contrast:** Maintain a minimum text contrast ratio of **4.5:1** (3:1 for large text) and non-text UI control contrast of **3:1** against adjacent backgrounds.
*   **Keyboard & Focus Management:** Provide high-visibility keyboard focus rings across all inputs and controls, ensuring keyboard users never get trapped in navigation loops.
*   **Tap Targets & Responsiveness:** Ensure all interactive touch targets are at least **24x24 pixels**. Verify that page layouts adapt smoothly without horizontal scrolling or text clipping down to 320px widths.

---

### 5. Implementation Governance & Structured UI Reviews

To prevent visual drift as products grow, establish automated guardrails and conduct structured interface audits.

#### A. Deploy Upstream & Downstream Guardrails
*   **Upstream Guardrails:** Load open-source design rule files (such as *Taste Skill*) into AI coding workflows prior to generation to enforce typographic, layout, and contrast rules.
*   **Downstream Auditing:** Run automated quality detectors (such as *Impeccable*) to scan compiled pages for 46+ slop patterns, unexpected border artifacts, or contrast failures before shipping.

#### B. Execute the 4-Question UI Audit Framework
Review every production interface using four concrete evaluation criteria:

1.  **What does this screen want someone to do, and is that the most important thing available?**  
    *Requirement:* The design must establish a clear primary visual focus, ensuring secondary actions are visibly quieter.
2.  **Which choices here are specific to this product, and which would work unchanged on someone else's?**  
    *Requirement:* Typography, color, imagery, and layout choices must directly reflect the unique product context rather than generic SaaS standards.
3.  **Has anyone opened every state, or only the one in the demo?**  
    *Requirement:* Inspect explicit screens for empty, loading, partial failure, permission denied, and validation error states.
4.  **Who is accountable for the decisions, and who reviewed the implementation?**  
    *Requirement:* Assign explicit ownership for design token systems and code implementation checks to ensure shipped software matches design specifications.

## 3. SECURITY

To make a website secure, you must implement defense-in-depth across transport encryption, server configurations, authentication, data handling, and infrastructure management. 

---

### 1. Enforce Sitewide Transport Security (HTTPS/TLS)
* **Encrypt All Traffic:** Secure all website traffic by enforcing sitewide HTTPS using Transport Layer Security (TLS) to prevent eavesdropping and Manipulator-in-the-Middle (MiTM) attacks.
* **Enforce Full Coverage:** Ensure every single route and asset is only served over HTTPS, as a single unencrypted page or form can compromise the entire application.
* **Modern Encryption & Ciphers:** Use 2048-bit SHA-256 certificates and explicitly disable outdated, insecure cipher suites (such as RC4 or weak Diffie-Hellman keys) on your web server.
* **Implement HSTS:** Enable **HTTP Strict Transport Security (HSTS)** with a long `max-age` duration to force browsers to automatically upgrade all HTTP requests to HTTPS, preventing downgrade and interception attacks.
* **Eliminate Mixed Content:** Verify that all active assets (such as JavaScript files, CSS, and media) are loaded over HTTPS to avoid security warnings and UI degradation.

---

### 2. Configure Security Headers & Server Hardening
* **Deploy Content Security Policy (CSP):** Implement a strict CSP using nonces or hashes to control which scripts and styles are allowed to execute, effectively neutralizing Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) threats.
* **Prevent Clickjacking:** Set the `frame-ancestors` directive within your CSP or use `X-Frame-Options` headers (`DENY` or `SAMEORIGIN`) to prevent unauthorized sites from framing your website.
* **Obscure Server Signatures:** Remove or obscure server identification headers (such as `X-Powered-By` or specific web server version tags) so attackers cannot target version-specific vulnerabilities.
* **Apply Least-Privilege Process Execution:** Configure web server processes to run under dedicated service accounts with minimal operating system privileges rather than root or Local System accounts.

---

### 3. Implement Secure Authentication & Credentials Hygiene
* **Hash Passwords Securely:** Never store plaintext passwords; store them using cryptographically strong, one-way salted and peppered algorithms such as **Argon2id** or **Scrypt**.
* **Protect Secrets & API Keys:** Store database credentials, private API keys, and environment variables in secure secrets managers outside the web root, never hardcoding them in client-side code or public prompts.
* **Multi-Factor Authentication & Rate Limiting:** Enforce Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA) for administrative and sensitive accounts, while implementing rate limiting and lockout policies on login endpoints to defeat brute-force and credential-stuffing attacks.
* **Harden Session Cookies:** Configure session cookies with the `HttpOnly` flag to prevent client-side scripts from stealing tokens via XSS, and the `Secure` flag so cookies transmit strictly over HTTPS.

---

### 4. Practice Server-Side Input Validation & Injection Defense
* **Validate All Inputs Server-Side:** Validate every user input, form field, and API payload on the server for syntax, data type, length, and acceptable range before processing.
* **Prevent SQL Injection:** Use parameterized queries (prepared statements) and stored procedures for all database queries rather than concatenating user input strings.
* **Contextual Output Encoding:** Contextually encode output data (such as HTML entity encoding) right before returning it to the client to ensure untrusted inputs are not rendered as executable script code.

---

### 5. Enforce Database Security & Server-Side Access Control
* **Enable Row-Level Security (RLS):** When using modern backend services (like PostgreSQL or Supabase), enforce Row-Level Security on all database tables so that public API keys cannot be exploited to read or write database content without authorization.
* **Server-Side Authorization Checks:** Perform all critical business logic and authorization checks (such as paywalls, user roles, and access boundaries) strictly on the server, as client-side checks can be easily manipulated via browser tools.
* **Principle of Least Privilege:** Restrict database accounts to only the specific permissions and tables necessary for their function.

---

### 6. Manage Infrastructure, Dependencies & Continuous Scans
* **Deploy Cloud DDoS & WAF Protection:** Utilize Web Application Firewalls (WAF) and Anycast DDoS mitigation services (such as Cloudflare or Akamai) alongside rate limiting to absorb volumetric traffic spikes and block malicious HTTP payloads.
* **Audit Open-Source Dependencies:** Maintain a Software Bill of Materials (SBOM) and routinely scan third-party libraries using Software Composition Analysis (SCA) tools (such as OWASP Dependency-Check or Snyk) to eliminate supply-chain vulnerabilities and hallucinated packages.
* **Self-Host Assets:** Download and self-host fonts and static libraries locally rather than linking directly to external CDNs to prevent leaking visitor IP data.
* **Regular Security Audits:** Conduct regular automated configuration scans, static/dynamic application security testing (SAST/DAST), and professional penetration tests prior to shipping code to production.


## 4. FUNCTIONS AND MARKETING

### UX Strategy & User Journey

1. **Conduct Continuous User Research:** Regularly schedule monthly interviews and analyze support tickets to validate real user frustrations.
2. **Map End-to-End Journeys:** Build flowcharts covering discovery, signup, onboarding, and support rather than optimizing screens in isolation.
3. **Accelerate Time-to-Value:** Design onboarding workflows that guide users to their first "aha moment" in under two minutes.
4. **Reduce Cognitive Load (Hick's Law):** Limit choices, set sensible defaults, and use progressive disclosure to prevent decision fatigue.
5. **Contextual Onboarding:** Display helpful tips only when relevant during a workflow rather than overwhelming users with linear upfront tours.
6. **Establish Team Exposure Hours:** Require every team member to observe live user research for at least two hours every six weeks.
7. **Allocate Debt Reduction Capacity:** Reserve 20% of team sprint capacity specifically for addressing technical and design debt.
8. **Prioritize via Digital Triage:** Evaluate incoming design requests using a repeatable scoring formula based on impact, effort, and risk.
9. **Map Aspirational Experiences:** Create journey maps of current friction points alongside target ideal states to align cross-functional stakeholders.
10. **Construct a Design Playbook:** Maintain a centralized repository of core UI patterns, component standards, and accessibility guidelines.
11. **Align Design Metrics with Business KPIs:** Map UX drivers (such as error rates and task speed) directly to business goals like revenue and support cost savings.
12. **Hold Weekly Dev-Design Overlaps:** Run short weekly "show-and-tell" sessions where designers and engineers review work-in-progress to align on feasibility.
13. **Run Usability Tests with 5 Real Users:** Test every new interface flow with five actual users to catch the vast majority of usability flaws early.
14. **Eliminate UX Dark Patterns:** Remove deceptive popups, pre-checked opt-ins, and hidden cancellation flows to foster long-term customer trust.
15. **Benchmark via the System Usability Scale (SUS):** Administer 10-question SUS surveys to quantify perceived usability and track improvements over time.

---

### UI Design, Layout & Visual Hierarchy

16. **Enforce a Strict Spacing Scale:** Apply a mathematically consistent spacing grid (such as 4px or 8px) across all layout containers.
17. **Establish Clear Button Hierarchy:** Limit button styles to one distinct visual pattern per role: primary, secondary, tertiary, and danger.
18. **Avoid Pure Black Text:** Set long-form body copy in dark grey (e.g., `#4F4F4F`) to improve reading comfort on light backgrounds.
19. **Restrain Color Palettes:** Use one primary brand color, one accent, and neutral backgrounds rather than chaotic color combinations.
20. **Use White Space Purposefully:** Leave generous negative space around key elements to create visual breathing room and emphasize calls-to-action.
21. **Select Distinctive Typography:** Pair a bold display font for headings with a readable sans-serif body typeface featuring taller lowercase x-heights.
22. **Set Proportional Line Height:** Increase line height as font sizes decrease (aiming for at least 1.5 line height on body text) to optimize legibility.
23. **Left-Align Paragraph Text:** Left-align body paragraphs and long text blocks to follow natural human reading patterns.
24. **Eliminate Decorative Emojis in Headings:** Replace functional emojis with proper SVG icons or descriptive text.
25. **Maintain Iconography Consistency:** Use icons that share a uniform visual weight, stroke width, and rendering style.
26. **Remove Unnecessary Visual Layering:** Eliminate redundant drop shadows, extra borders, and background fills on single card elements.
27. **Label Action Buttons Explicitly:** Use clear, specific action labels (e.g., "Delete Location") rather than ambiguous phrases (e.g., "Yes").
28. **Provide Visible Event Feedback:** Design interactive controls with explicit hover, focus, active, disabled, and loading visual states.
29. **Avoid Color-Only Status Cues:** Pair colors with text labels, icons, or textures so visual indicators remain accessible to colorblind users.
30. **Ensure Interactive Touch Targets:** Size all clickable buttons, input fields, and links to a minimum tap target of 24x24 pixels (44x44px on mobile).
31. **Design Uncomfortable UI States:** Systematically create explicit UI designs for empty states, loading skeletons, partial failures, validation errors, and permission denials.
32. **Avoid Deeply Nested Containers:** Refrain from wrapping single lines of text inside nested bordered cards.
33. **Limit Uppercase Text:** Restrict all-caps typography to brief eyebrow labels to prevent text scanning fatigue.
34. **Strip Out Left-Border Card Accent Lines:** Remove decorative 4px left-border stripes from card containers.
35. **Reduce Em Dash Frequency:** Replace excessive long em dashes (—) with standard punctuation for clearer, natural sentence flow.

---

### Content Strategy, Authenticity & Copywriting

36. **Draft a 4-Part Brief:** Define brand name, specific offer, target audience, and primary page goal before generating copy.
37. **Write Conversational Product Copy:** Replace generic buzzwords ("seamless", "empower", "elevate", "cutting-edge") with exact user terminology.
38. **Pass the "Swap Test":** Ensure homepage copy contains specific numbers, dates, prices, and locations so it cannot be copy-pasted onto a competitor's site.
39. **Feature Authentic Photography:** Replace synthetic stock humans and abstract 3D graphics with real photos of your team, premises, and actual products.
40. **Showcase Verified Social Proof:** Feature client testimonials that include full names, verified roles, company logos, and real faces.
41. **Structure Content Around Buying Questions:** Sequence page sections to directly answer the natural questions buyers ask before purchasing.
42. **Design with Realistic Content:** Use actual text lengths during wireframing to prevent layout breaks when real copy replaces placeholder text.
43. **Remove Over-Explaining Copy:** Strip out unnecessary instructional paragraphs accompanying self-explanatory controls or cards.
44. **Vary Sentence and Paragraph Rhythm:** Avoid monotonous sentence structures by mixing short and long phrases naturally.
45. **Write Outcome-Focused Headlines:** Craft headlines that state the target audience and specific outcome achieved rather than broad platitudes.
46. **Display Real UI Workflows:** Show high-resolution screenshots of actual software interfaces instead of stylized 3D floating dashboards.
47. **Self-Host Brand Fonts:** Download and serve font files directly from your web server to protect visitor IP privacy.
48. **Apply the TASL Attribution Framework:** When crediting third-party media, state Title, Author, Source, and Link while disclosing modifications.
49. **Include Legal & Advice Disclaimers:** Clarify that website content is for informational purposes rather than professional guidance.
50. **Maintain Geographical Content Localization:** Match spellings, currency symbols, address formats, and phone numbers to your target market.

---

### Conversion Rate Optimization & Customer Engagement

51. **Position Social Proof Adjacent to CTAs:** Place client reviews or trust badges directly next to primary signup and checkout buttons to boost conversion.
52. **Streamline Checkout & Signup Forms:** Eliminate non-essential input fields and implement inline validation to minimize form drop-off.
53. **Deploy Micro-Interactions:** Add subtle, immediate visual feedback (like checkmark confirmations or progress bars) upon completing key tasks.
54. **Implement Intelligent Site Search:** Integrate site-wide search powered by Natural Language Processing (NLP) to handle typos and conversational queries.
55. **Use Specific Call-to-Action Labels:** Write action-oriented button text ("Get My Free Trial") rather than generic terms ("Submit").
56. **Diversify Customer Contact Channels:** Provide multiple contact methods (chat, web forms, phone) to accommodate user preferences.
57. **Actively Respond to Online Reviews:** Collect first- and third-party customer reviews and respond to them consistently to improve brand sentiment.
58. **Maintain a Fresh Content/Resource Hub:** Regularly update blog posts and resource hubs focusing on specific audience pain points.
59. **Publish Explicit E-Commerce Policies:** Clearly display refund, return, shipping, and tax policies prior to purchase.
60. **Include a Dedicated "About Us" Page:** Build human connection by detailing your business background, team, and core values.
61. **Provide Ample Touch Target Spacing:** Add generous padding between adjacent links and action buttons to prevent accidental clicks on mobile.
62. **Display Business Verification Info:** Prominently post company addresses, phone numbers, and official registration numbers in the site footer.
63. **Run A/B Tests on Key Pages:** Test variations of high-impact landing pages using event analytics before committing to full site overhauls.
64. **Keep Main Navigation Predictable:** Organize primary navigation menus logically, enabling visitors to reach core pages within three clicks.
65. **Provide Error Recovery Paths:** Write concise error modals that explain what happened, why, and offer a single direct CTA to try again.

---

### Performance, Technical Architecture & SEO

66. **Enforce Sitewide HTTPS:** Automatically redirect all unencrypted HTTP requests to secure HTTPS across all routes and subdomains.
67. **Optimize Core Web Vitals:** Maintain LCP under 2.5s, INP under 200ms, and minimize layout shifts (CLS) to satisfy search ranking factors.
68. **Implement Schema Markup:** Embed structured schema data for videos, images, FAQs, and reviews to earn rich search snippets.
69. **Build Mobile-First Layouts:** Ensure site layouts adapt dynamically down to 320px screen widths without horizontal scrollbars.
70. **Structure Clean URLs:** Maintain short, descriptive, keyword-rich URL paths that reflect clear site hierarchy.
71. **Deploy Content Security Policy (CSP):** Configure robust CSP headers to restrict unauthorized script execution and neutralize XSS vulnerabilities.
72. **Utilize Multi-Layered Caching:** Deploy edge CDNs for static assets and Redis/Memcached for database queries to minimize latency.
73. **Eliminate Mixed Content Warnings:** Ensure all passive images and active JavaScript assets load exclusively over HTTPS.
74. **Enable HSTS Preloading:** Set `Strict-Transport-Security` headers with long `max-age` durations to force HTTPS connections.
75. **Compress & Optimize Web Media:** Serve modern compressed image formats, enable lazy loading, and use responsive picture elements.
76. **Maintain Semantic HTML Markup:** Structure page documents using proper semantic markup (`<header>`, `<main>`, `<nav>`, `<footer>`).
77. **Optimize Content for Voice Queries:** Format FAQ sections and metadata using natural, long-tail conversational phrasing.
78. **Decouple Content from Presentation:** Keep HTML structural content strictly separated from CSS presentation styles to optimize caching and multi-device delivery.
79. **Implement API Versioning:** Version backend APIs (`/v1/`, `/v2/`) to prevent frontend interface breaks during upgrades.
80. **Continuously Monitor Site Performance:** Track crash rates, load times, and network errors alongside session replays to identify regressions.

---

### Security, Credibility & Legal Compliance

81. **Publish a Compliant Privacy Policy:** Maintain an easily accessible privacy policy outlining data collection, usage, and sharing, updating it annually.
82. **Publish Terms of Service (ToS):** Post clear Terms of Use governing site rules, intellectual property rights, liability limits, and legal jurisdiction.
83. **Deploy Affirmative Cookie Banners (GDPR):** Require explicit user opt-in consent before executing non-essential tracking cookies for EU visitors.
84. **Provide Data Opt-Out Options (CCPA):** Include a clear "Do Not Sell or Share My Personal Information" link for U.S. visitors.
85. **Meet WCAG 2.2 AA Conformance:** Audit and remediate website code to meet international web accessibility standards.
86. **Maintain Minimum Color Contrast Ratios:** Ensure small text meets at least 4.5:1 contrast, large text meets 3:1, and UI controls meet 3:1 against backgrounds.
87. **Ensure Full Keyboard Navigation:** Verify that all navigation links, buttons, and form controls are fully operable using a keyboard alone.
88. **Provide High-Visibility Focus Rings:** Display clear visual focus indicators when navigating interactive controls via keyboard.
89. **Provide Descriptive Alt Text for Images:** Include accurate `alt` text descriptions for all non-decorative imagery.
90. **Label Form Fields Programmatically:** Use explicit `<label>` elements associated with form inputs rather than relying solely on visual placeholder text.
91. **Publish an Accessibility Statement:** Post an accessibility statement detailing compliance standards met, known limitations, and contact details.
92. **Hash Passwords Securely (Argon2id/Scrypt):** Store passwords using salted, cryptographically strong one-way hashing algorithms.
93. **Enforce Server-Side Input Validation:** Validate all input fields server-side for syntax, data type, and length to block SQL injection and malicious scripts.
94. **Protect API Keys and Tokens:** Keep private API keys out of client-side code bundles and store them securely in server environment variables.
95. **Enable Row-Level Security (RLS):** Configure Row-Level Security on databases so client keys cannot access unauthorized rows.
96. **Set Secure Cookie Flags (`HttpOnly` & `Secure`):** Configure session cookies with `HttpOnly` and `Secure` attributes to block script-based cookie theft.
97. **Enforce Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA):** Require MFA for administrative dashboards and sensitive account management access.
98. **Register a DMCA Designated Agent:** Register an official agent with the U.S. Copyright Office to protect safe harbor status for user content.
99. **Comply with Anti-SPAM Laws (CAN-SPAM):** Include accurate email headers, a valid physical business address, and an instant opt-out mechanism in all marketing communications.
100. **Display Clear Auto-Renewal Disclosures:** Present subscription prices, billing frequencies, and cancellation links directly adjacent to purchase buttons.

## 5. CODE
### Add the features listed below:

## Integrate the <DitherVeil /> component from React Bits

You are helping integrate an open-source React component into an existing application.

### Component: DitherVeil
### Variant: JavaScript + CSS
### Dependencies: ogl

---

### Usage Example
```jsx
import DitherVeil from './DitherVeil';

<div style={{ width: '100%', height: '600px', position: 'relative' }}>
  <DitherVeil
    src="https://images.unsplash.com/photo-1737071371043-761e02b1ef95?q=80&w=1400&auto=format&fit=crop"
    pattern="floyd"
    pixelSize={2}
    inkColor="#120f17"
    paperColor="#f4f1ea"
    revealRadius={200}
    softness={0.6}
    linger={1}
  />
</div>
```

### Props
| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
|------|------|---------|-------------|
| src | string | 'https://images.unsplash.com/...' | Image to veil. Must be served with CORS headers. |
| fit | 'contain' | 'cover' | 'contain' | Show the whole image on the ink, or crop it to fill the container. |
| pattern | 'bayer' | 'noise' | 'atkinson' | 'floyd' | 'lines' | 'floyd' | How the image is broken into dots: an ordered Bayer grid, blue noise stipple, Atkinson or Floyd-Steinberg error diffusion, or diagonal engraving lines. |
| palette | 'duotone' | 'rgb' | 'duotone' | Duotone maps brightness between the ink and paper colours. RGB dithers each channel for a retro colour look. |
| pixelSize | number | 2 | Size of each dither cell, in px. |
| levels | number | 2 | Tones per channel. 2 is pure 1-bit, higher values add in-between shades. |
| inkColor | string | '#120f17' | Colour of the darkest tone. |
| paperColor | string | '#f4f1ea' | Colour of the lightest tone. |
| contrast | number | 1.15 | Tonal contrast applied before dithering. |
| brightness | number | 0 | Shifts the image lighter or darker before dithering. |
| revealRadius | number | 200 | Radius of the full-colour window around the cursor, in px. |
| softness | number | 0.6 | How much of the reveal edge dissolves through the dither. 0 is a hard cut. |
| linger | number | 1 | Seconds the revealed trail takes to knit back into dither. 0 turns the trail off. |
| rimColor | string | '#a78bfa' | Colour of the cells along the dissolving edge. |
| rim | number | 0 | Thickness of the coloured rim on the dissolving edge. 0 hides it. |
| reverse | boolean | false | Start in full colour and dither wherever the cursor goes. |
| wander | boolean | false | Let the reveal drift around on its own while the pointer is away. |
| clickBurst | boolean | true | Clicking sends a ring of colour rippling out across the image. |
| className | string | '' | Extra classes on the container. |
| style | CSSProperties | - | Inline styles on the container. |

### Full Component Source
```jsx
'use client';

import { useEffect, useRef } from 'react';
import { Renderer, Program, Mesh, Triangle, Texture, RenderTarget } from 'ogl';

import './DitherVeil.css';

const DEFAULT_SRC = 'https://images.unsplash.com/photo-1737071371043-761e02b1ef95?q=80&w=1400&auto=format&fit=crop';

const ORDERED = { bayer: 0, noise: 1, lines: 2 };

const KERNELS = {
  atkinson: [
    [1, 0, 1 / 8],
    [2, 0, 1 / 8],
    [-1, 1, 1 / 8],
    [0, 1, 1 / 8],
    [1, 1, 1 / 8],
    [0, 2, 1 / 8]
  ],
  floyd: [
    [1, 0, 7 / 16],
    [-1, 1, 3 / 16],
    [0, 1, 5 / 16],
    [1, 1, 1 / 16]
  ]
};

const MASK_SCALE = 0.5;
const MAX_BURSTS = 4;
const BURST_SECONDS = 1.2;
const HOLD = 1.6;
const INTRO_MS = 1100;

const hexToRgb = hex => {
  let h = String(hex || '').replace('#', '');
  if (h.length === 3) h = h.replace(/./g, c => c + c);
  const n = parseInt(h.slice(0, 6), 16);
  return Number.isNaN(n) ? [0, 0, 0] : [((n >> 16) & 255) / 255, ((n >> 8) & 255) / 255, (n & 255) / 255];
};

const fitScale = (w, h, iw, ih, contain) => {
  const ratio = w / h / (iw / ih);
  if (contain) return ratio > 1 ? [ratio, 1] : [1, 1 / ratio];
  return ratio > 1 ? [1, 1 / ratio] : [ratio, 1];
};

const wanderAt = (t, w, h) => [
  w * (0.5 + 0.33 * Math.sin(t * 0.53) + 0.08 * Math.sin(t * 1.31 + 0.6)),
  h * (0.5 + 0.28 * Math.sin(t * 0.71 + 1.1) + 0.07 * Math.cos(t * 1.57))
];

const measureEdge = (context, image) => {
  const size = 32;
  context.canvas.width = size;
  context.canvas.height = size;
  context.drawImage(image, 0, 0, size, size);
  const data = context.getImageData(0, 0, size, size).data;
  const sum = [0, 0, 0];
  const squares = [0, 0, 0];
  let count = 0;
  for (let y = 0; y < size; y++) {
    for (let x = 0; x < size; x++) {
      if (x > 0 && y > 0 && x < size - 1 && y < size - 1) continue;
      for (let c = 0; c < 3; c++) {
        const v = data[(y * size + x) * 4 + c] / 255;
        sum[c] += v;
        squares[c] += v * v;
      }
      count++;
    }
  }
  const matte = [sum[0] / count, sum[1] / count, sum[2] / count];
  const spread =
    matte.reduce((total, mean, c) => total + Math.sqrt(Math.max(0, squares[c] / count - mean * mean)), 0) / 3;
  return { matte, plain: spread < 0.06 };
};

let blueNoise = null;

const getBlueNoise = () => {
  if (blueNoise) return blueNoise;
  const size = 64;
  const n = size * size;
  const wrap = size - 1;
  const taps = [];
  for (let dy = -6; dy <= 6; dy++) {
    for (let dx = -6; dx <= 6; dx++) taps.push(dx, dy, Math.exp(-(dx * dx + dy * dy) / 4.5));
  }
  const energy = new Float32Array(n);
  const on = new Uint8Array(n);
  const splat = (i, sign) => {
    const x = i % size;
    const y = (i - x) / size;
    for (let k = 0; k < taps.length; k += 3) {
      energy[((y + taps[k + 1]) & wrap) * size + ((x + taps[k]) & wrap)] += sign * taps[k + 2];
    }
  };
  const find = (state, highest) => {
    let best = 0;
    let value = highest ? -Infinity : Infinity;
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
      if (on[i] !== state) continue;
      if (highest ? energy[i] > value : energy[i] < value) {
        value = energy[i];
        best = i;
      }
    }
    return best;
  };
  let seed = 7;
  const random = () => {
    seed = (seed * 16807) % 2147483647;
    return seed / 2147483647;
  };
  const initial = Math.round(n * 0.1);
  for (let placed = 0; placed < initial; ) {
    const i = Math.floor(random() * n);
    if (on[i]) continue;
    on[i] = 1;
    splat(i, 1);
    placed++;
  }
  for (let guard = 0; guard < n; guard++) {
    const cluster = find(1, true);
    on[cluster] = 0;
    splat(cluster, -1);
    const gap = find(0, false);
    on[gap] = 1;
    splat(gap, 1);
    if (gap === cluster) break;
  }
  const seedOn = on.slice();
  const seedEnergy = energy.slice();
  const rank = new Uint16Array(n);
  for (let r = initial - 1; r >= 0; r--) {
    const cluster = find(1, true);
    on[cluster] = 0;
    splat(cluster, -1);
    rank[cluster] = r;
  }
  on.set(seedOn);
  energy.set(seedEnergy);
  for (let r = initial; r < n; r++) {
    const gap = find(0, false);
    on[gap] = 1;
    splat(gap, 1);
    rank[gap] = r;
  }
  const data = new Uint8Array(n * 4);
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    const v = Math.floor((rank[i] / n) * 256);
    data[i * 4] = v;
    data[i * 4 + 1] = v;
    data[i * 4 + 2] = v;
    data[i * 4 + 3] = 255;
  }
  blueNoise = data;
  return data;
};

const diffuse = (pixels, cols, rows, kernel, levels, rgb, grade) => {
  const channels = rgb ? 3 : 1;
  const values = new Float32Array(cols * rows * channels);
  for (let i = 0; i < cols * rows; i++) {
    const r = pixels[i * 4] / 255;
    const g = pixels[i * 4 + 1] / 255;
    const b = pixels[i * 4 + 2] / 255;
    if (rgb) {
      values[i * 3] = grade(r);
      values[i * 3 + 1] = grade(g);
      values[i * 3 + 2] = grade(b);
    } else {
      values[i] = grade(0.2126 * r + 0.7152 * g + 0.0722 * b);
    }
  }
  const steps = levels - 1;
  const out = new Uint8Array(cols * rows * 4);
  for (let y = 0; y < rows; y++) {
    const dir = y & 1 ? -1 : 1;
    for (let i = 0; i < cols; i++) {
      const x = dir > 0 ? i : cols - 1 - i;
      const p = y * cols + x;
      for (let c = 0; c < channels; c++) {
        const old = values[p * channels + c];
        const q = Math.min(steps, Math.max(0, Math.round(old * steps))) / steps;
        const err = old - q;
        const byte = Math.round(q * 255);
        if (rgb) out[p * 4 + c] = byte;
        else out[p * 4] = out[p * 4 + 1] = out[p * 4 + 2] = byte;
        for (let k = 0; k < kernel.length; k++) {
          const nx = x + kernel[k][0] * dir;
          const ny = y + kernel[k][1];
          if (nx < 0 || nx >= cols || ny >= rows) continue;
          values[(ny * cols + nx) * channels + c] += err * kernel[k][2];
        }
      }
      out[p * 4 + 3] = 255;
    }
  }
  return out;
};

const vertex = `#version 300 es
in vec2 position;
in vec2 uv;
out vec2 vUv;
void main() {
  vUv = uv;
  gl_Position = vec4(position, 0.0, 1.0);
}
`;

const maskFragment = `#version 300 es
precision highp float;

uniform sampler2D tPrev;
uniform vec2 uSize;
uniform vec2 uFrom;
uniform vec2 uTo;
uniform float uRadius;
uniform float uSoftness;
uniform float uStrength;
uniform float uFade;
uniform float uHold;

in vec2 vUv;
out vec4 fragColor;

float strokeDistance(vec2 p, vec2 a, vec2 b) {
  vec2 ab = b - a;
  float h = clamp(dot(p - a, ab) / max(dot(ab, ab), 0.0001), 0.0, 1.0);
  return length(p - a - ab * h);
}

void main() {
  vec2 p = vec2(vUv.x, 1.0 - vUv.y) * uSize;
  float trail = max(texture(tPrev, vUv).r - uFade, 0.0);
  float band = max(uRadius * uSoftness, 1.0) * uHold;
  float d = strokeDistance(p, uFrom, uTo);
  trail = max(trail, clamp((uRadius - d) / band, 0.0, 1.0) * uStrength);
  fragColor = vec4(trail, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0);
}
`;

const viewFragment = `#version 300 es
precision highp float;
precision highp int;

uniform sampler2D tImage;
uniform sampler2D tMask;
uniform sampler2D tNoise;
uniform sampler2D tDiffused;
uniform vec2 uResolution;
uniform vec2 uCover;
uniform float uLod;
uniform float uCell;
uniform int uPattern;
uniform int uPalette;
uniform float uLevels;
uniform vec3 uInk;
uniform vec3 uPaper;
uniform vec3 uRimColor;
uniform float uRim;
uniform float uContrast;
uniform float uBrightness;
uniform float uReverse;
uniform float uIntro;
uniform float uHold;
uniform vec3 uMatte;
uniform float uKey;
uniform vec2 uSize;
uniform vec4 uBursts[4];
uniform float uBurstWidth;

in vec2 vUv;
out vec4 fragColor;

float bayer(vec2 cell) {
  ivec2 p = ivec2(mod(cell, 8.0));
  int v = p.x ^ p.y;
  int m = ((v & 1) << 5) | ((p.y & 1) << 4) | ((v & 2) << 2) | ((p.y & 2) << 1) | ((v & 4) >> 1) | ((p.y & 4) >> 2);
  return (float(m) + 0.5) / 64.0;
}

float blueNoise(vec2 cell) {
  return (texelFetch(tNoise, ivec2(mod(cell, 64.0)), 0).r * 255.0 + 0.5) / 256.0;
}

float engraving(vec2 cell) {
  float period = 6.0;
  float f = (mod(cell.x + cell.y, period) + 0.5) / period;
  return clamp(abs(f * 2.0 - 1.0) + (bayer(cell) - 0.5) * (2.0 / period), 0.0, 1.0);
}

vec2 imageUv(vec2 uv) {
  return (uv - 0.5) * uCover + 0.5;
}

float within(vec2 p) {
  vec2 s = step(vec2(0.0), p) * step(p, vec2(1.0));
  return s.x * s.y;
}

vec3 grade(vec3 c) {
  return pow(clamp((c - 0.5) * uContrast + 0.5 + uBrightness, 0.0, 1.0), vec3(1.6));
}

float shockwave(vec2 p) {
  float value = 0.0;
  for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++) {
    vec4 burst = uBursts[i];
    if (burst.w <= 0.0) continue;
    float offset = distance(p, burst.xy) - burst.z;
    float edge = offset > 0.0 ? offset / (uBurstWidth * 0.35) : -offset / uBurstWidth;
    value = max(value, clamp(1.0 - edge, 0.0, 1.0) * burst.w);
  }
  return value;
}

vec3 toned(vec3 c) {
  return uPalette == 1 ? grade(c) : grade(vec3(dot(c, vec3(0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722))));
}

vec3 quantize(vec3 v, float t) {
  float steps = max(uLevels - 1.0, 1.0);
  vec3 s = v * steps;
  vec3 base = floor(s);
  return min(base + step(vec3(t), s - base), vec3(steps)) / steps;
}

void main() {
  vec2 px = vec2(gl_FragCoord.x, uResolution.y - gl_FragCoord.y);
  vec2 cell = floor(px / uCell);
  vec2 center = (cell + 0.5) * uCell;
  vec2 cellUv = vec2(center.x / uResolution.x, 1.0 - center.y / uResolution.y);

  vec2 sampleUv = imageUv(cellUv);
  float framed = within(sampleUv);
  vec3 level;
  if (uPattern == 3) {
    level = texelFetch(tDiffused, ivec2(cell), 0).rgb;
  } else {
    vec3 c = mix(uMatte, textureLod(tImage, sampleUv, uLod).rgb, framed);
    float t = uPattern == 1 ? blueNoise(cell) : (uPattern == 2 ? engraving(cell) : bayer(cell));
    level = quantize(toned(c), t);
  }

  vec3 color = mix(uInk, mix(uInk, uPaper, level), max(framed, uKey));
  vec3 backdrop = mix(uInk, uPaper, toned(uMatte));
  vec2 photoUv = imageUv(vUv);
  vec3 raw = texture(tImage, photoUv).rgb;
  float plain = uKey * (1.0 - smoothstep(0.05, 0.22, distance(raw, uMatte)));
  vec3 photo = mix(mix(uInk, backdrop, uKey), mix(raw, backdrop, plain), within(photoUv));

  vec2 point = vec2(cellUv.x, 1.0 - cellUv.y) * uSize;
  float mask = max(clamp(texture(tMask, cellUv).r * uHold, 0.0, 1.0), shockwave(point));
  float shown = mix(mask, 1.0 - mask, uReverse);
  float order = bayer(cell.yx);
  float low = order * (1.0 - uRim);
  color = mix(color, uRimColor, step(low, shown) * step(0.001, uRim));
  color = mix(color, photo, step(low + uRim, shown));

  vec2 aspect = vec2(uResolution.x / uResolution.y, 1.0);
  float spread = length((cellUv - 0.5) * aspect) / length(aspect * 0.5);
  float appear = step(spread * 0.72 + bayer(cell + vec2(3.0, 5.0)) * 0.28, uIntro * 1.001);
  fragColor = vec4(mix(uInk, color, appear), 1.0);
}
`;

const DitherVeil = ({
  src = DEFAULT_SRC,
  fit = 'contain',
  pattern = 'floyd',
  pixelSize = 2,
  levels = 2,
  palette = 'duotone',
  inkColor = '#120f17',
  paperColor = '#f4f1ea',
  contrast = 1.15,
  brightness = 0,
  revealRadius = 200,
  softness = 0.6,
  linger = 1,
  rimColor = '#a78bfa',
  rim = 0,
  reverse = false,
  wander = false,
  clickBurst = true,
  className = '',
  style
}) => {
  const containerRef = useRef(null);
  const settingsRef = useRef(null);
  const wakeRef = useRef(() => {});

  useEffect(() => {
    settingsRef.current = {
      fit,
      pattern,
      pixelSize,
      levels,
      palette,
      inkColor,
      paperColor,
      contrast,
      brightness,
      revealRadius,
      softness,
      linger,
      rimColor,
      rim,
      reverse,
      wander,
      clickBurst
    };
    wakeRef.current();
  });

  useEffect(() => {
    const container = containerRef.current;
    if (!container) return undefined;

    const reducedMotion = window.matchMedia?.('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;
    const renderer = new Renderer({ dpr: Math.min(window.devicePixelRatio || 1, 2), alpha: false, antialias: false });
    const gl = renderer.gl;
    const canvas = gl.canvas;
    canvas.style.display = 'block';
    canvas.style.width = '100%';
    canvas.style.height = '100%';
    container.appendChild(canvas);

    const floatMask = renderer.isWebgl2 && !!renderer.getExtension('EXT_color_buffer_float');
    const geometry = new Triangle(gl);

    const dataTexture = () =>
      new Texture(gl, {
        image: new Uint8Array(4),
        width: 1,
        height: 1,
        generateMipmaps: false,
        flipY: false,
        minFilter: gl.NEAREST,
        magFilter: gl.NEAREST
      });
    const imageTexture = new Texture(gl, { minFilter: gl.LINEAR_MIPMAP_LINEAR, magFilter: gl.LINEAR });
    const noiseTexture = dataTexture();
    const diffusedTexture = dataTexture();

    const createMask = (w, h) =>
      new RenderTarget(gl, {
        width: w,
        height: h,
        depth: false,
        type: floatMask ? gl.HALF_FLOAT : gl.UNSIGNED_BYTE,
        internalFormat: floatMask ? gl.RGBA16F : gl.RGBA,
        minFilter: gl.LINEAR,
        magFilter: gl.LINEAR
      });
    const destroyMask = target => {
      gl.deleteFramebuffer(target.buffer);
      gl.deleteTexture(target.texture.texture);
    };
    let masks = [createMask(2, 2), createMask(2, 2)];

    const maskUniforms = {
      tPrev: { value: masks[0].texture },
      uSize: { value: [1, 1] },
      uFrom: { value: [0, 0] },
      uTo: { value: [0, 0] },
      uRadius: { value: 1 },
      uSoftness: { value: 0.5 },
      uStrength: { value: 0 },
      uFade: { value: 1 },
      uHold: { value: HOLD }
    };
    const viewUniforms = {
      tImage: { value: imageTexture },
      tMask: { value: masks[0].texture },
      tNoise: { value: noiseTexture },
      tDiffused: { value: diffusedTexture },
      uResolution: { value: [1, 1] },
      uCover: { value: [1, 1] },
      uLod: { value: 0 },
      uCell: { value: 3 },
      uPattern: { value: 0 },
      uPalette: { value: 0 },
      uLevels: { value: 2 },
      uInk: { value: [0, 0, 0] },
      uPaper: { value: [1, 1, 1] },
      uRimColor: { value: [1, 1, 1] },
      uRim: { value: 0 },
      uContrast: { value: 1 },
      uBrightness: { value: 0 },
      uReverse: { value: 0 },
      uIntro: { value: 0 },
      uHold: { value: HOLD },
      uMatte: { value: [0, 0, 0] },
      uKey: { value: 0 },
      uSize: { value: [1, 1] },
      uBursts: { value: Array.from({ length: MAX_BURSTS * 4 }, () => 0) },
      uBurstWidth: { value: 60 }
    };
    const program = (fragment, uniforms) =>
      new Program(gl, { vertex, fragment, uniforms, depthTest: false, depthWrite: false });
    const maskMesh = new Mesh(gl, { geometry, program: program(maskFragment, maskUniforms) });
    const viewMesh = new Mesh(gl, { geometry, program: program(viewFragment, viewUniforms) });

    const sampler = document.createElement('canvas');
    const samplerContext = sampler.getContext('2d', { willReadFrequently: true });

    let image = null;
    let introStart = 0;
    let diffusedKey = '';
    let diffusionBlocked = false;
    let width = 1;
    let height = 1;
    let visible = true;
    let raf = 0;
    let last = performance.now();
    let trailUntil = 0;
    let presence = 0;
    let drift = 0;
    const pointer = { x: 0, y: 0, inside: false, fresh: true, placed: false };
    const brush = { x: 0, y: 0, px: 0, py: 0 };
    const bursts = [];

    let resizePending = false;
    const applyLayout = () => {
      width = Math.max(1, container.clientWidth);
      height = Math.max(1, container.clientHeight);
      renderer.setSize(width, height);
      viewUniforms.uResolution.value = [canvas.width, canvas.height];
      maskUniforms.uSize.value = [width, height];
      viewUniforms.uSize.value = [width, height];
      const mw = Math.max(2, Math.round(width * MASK_SCALE));
      const mh = Math.max(2, Math.round(height * MASK_SCALE));
      if (mw !== masks[0].width || mh !== masks[0].height) {
        const previous = masks;
        masks = [createMask(mw, mh), createMask(mw, mh)];
        maskUniforms.tPrev.value = previous[0].texture;
        maskUniforms.uStrength.value = 0;
        maskUniforms.uFade.value = 0;
        renderer.render({ scene: maskMesh, target: masks[0] });
        previous.forEach(destroyMask);
      }
      if (!pointer.placed) {
        pointer.x = width / 2;
        pointer.y = height / 2;
      }
    };

    const layout = () => {
      resizePending = true;
      wake();
    };

    const updateDiffusion = (cell, s) => {
      if (!image || !samplerContext) return;
      const cols = Math.ceil(canvas.width / cell);
      const rows = Math.ceil(canvas.height / cell);
      const key = [
        s.pattern,
        s.levels,
        s.palette,
        s.contrast,
        s.brightness,
        s.fit,
        cols,
        rows,
        canvas.width,
        canvas.height
      ].join('|');
      if (key === diffusedKey) return;
      diffusedKey = key;
      const [cx, cy] = viewUniforms.uCover.value;
      const iw = image.naturalWidth;
      const ih = image.naturalHeight;
      sampler.width = cols;
      sampler.height = rows;
      samplerContext.imageSmoothingEnabled = true;
      samplerContext.imageSmoothingQuality = 'high';
      const [mr, mg, mb] = viewUniforms.uMatte.value;
      samplerContext.fillStyle = `rgb(${mr * 255}, ${mg * 255}, ${mb * 255})`;
      samplerContext.fillRect(0, 0, cols, rows);
      const sx = (0.5 - 0.5 * cx) * iw;
      const sy = (0.5 - 0.5 * cy) * ih;
      const sw = ((cols * cell) / canvas.width) * cx * iw;
      const sh = ((rows * cell) / canvas.height) * cy * ih;
      const x0 = Math.max(sx, 0);
      const y0 = Math.max(sy, 0);
      const x1 = Math.min(sx + sw, iw);
      const y1 = Math.min(sy + sh, ih);
      if (x1 > x0 && y1 > y0) {
        samplerContext.drawImage(
          image,
          x0,
          y0,
          x1 - x0,
          y1 - y0,
          ((x0 - sx) / sw) * cols,
          ((y0 - sy) / sh) * rows,
          ((x1 - x0) / sw) * cols,
          ((y1 - y0) / sh) * rows
        );
      }
      const grade = v => Math.pow(Math.min(1, Math.max(0, (v - 0.5) * s.contrast + 0.5 + s.brightness)), 1.6);
      const pixels = samplerContext.getImageData(0, 0, cols, rows).data;
      diffusedTexture.image = diffuse(pixels, cols, rows, KERNELS[s.pattern], s.levels, s.palette === 'rgb', grade);
      diffusedTexture.width = cols;
      diffusedTexture.height = rows;
      diffusedTexture.needsUpdate = true;
    };

    const frame = now => {
      raf = 0;
      if (resizePending) {
        resizePending = false;
        applyLayout();
      }
      const s = settingsRef.current;
      if (!s) return;
      const dt = Math.min(0.05, Math.max(0, (now - last) / 1000));
      last = now;

      const wanderOn = s.wander && !reducedMotion;
      let targetX = pointer.x;
      let targetY = pointer.y;
      if (!pointer.inside && wanderOn) {
        drift = Math.min(1, drift + dt / 1.2);
        const [wx, wy] = wanderAt(now / 1000, width, height);
        const k = drift * drift * (3 - 2 * drift);
        targetX += (wx - targetX) * k;
        targetY += (wy - targetY) * k;
      } else {
        drift = 0;
      }

      presence += ((pointer.inside || wanderOn ? 1 : 0) - presence) * (1 - Math.exp(-dt / 0.16));

      if (pointer.fresh) {
        brush.x = brush.px = targetX;
        brush.y = brush.py = targetY;
        pointer.fresh = false;
      } else {
        const follow = 1 - Math.exp(-dt / 0.035);
        brush.x += (targetX - brush.x) * follow;
        brush.y += (targetY - brush.y) * follow;
      }

      const burstData = viewUniforms.uBursts.value;
      burstData.fill(0);
      for (let i = bursts.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
        if ((now - bursts[i].start) / 1000 >= BURST_SECONDS) bursts.splice(i, 1);
      }
      bursts.forEach((b, i) => {
        const k = Math.max(0, (now - b.start) / 1000 / BURST_SECONDS);
        const reach =
          Math.hypot(Math.max(b.x, width - b.x), Math.max(b.y, height - b.y)) + viewUniforms.uBurstWidth.value;
        burstData[i * 4] = b.x;
        burstData[i * 4 + 1] = b.y;
        burstData[i * 4 + 2] = reach * Math.sin((k * Math.PI) / 2);
        burstData[i * 4 + 3] = 1 - k * k * k;
      });

      if (presence > 0.002) trailUntil = now + s.linger * 1000 + 150;

      const minFade = floatMask ? 0 : 1.5 / 255;
      const fade = s.linger > 0 ? dt / s.linger : 1;
      maskUniforms.tPrev.value = masks[0].texture;
      maskUniforms.uFrom.value = [brush.px, brush.py];
      maskUniforms.uTo.value = [brush.x, brush.y];
      maskUniforms.uRadius.value = s.revealRadius * (0.45 + 0.55 * presence);
      maskUniforms.uSoftness.value = s.softness;
      maskUniforms.uStrength.value = presence;
      maskUniforms.uFade.value = Math.max(fade, minFade);
      viewUniforms.uBurstWidth.value = Math.max(60, s.revealRadius * 0.9);
      renderer.render({ scene: maskMesh, target: masks[1] });
      masks.reverse();
      brush.px = brush.x;
      brush.py = brush.y;

      const cell = Math.max(1, Math.round(s.pixelSize * renderer.dpr));
      if (image) {
        viewUniforms.uCover.value = fitScale(
          canvas.width,
          canvas.height,
          image.naturalWidth,
          image.naturalHeight,
          s.fit === 'contain'
        );
      }
      let patternIndex = ORDERED[s.pattern] ?? 0;
      if (KERNELS[s.pattern]) {
        patternIndex = 0;
        if (image && !diffusionBlocked) {
          try {
            updateDiffusion(cell, s);
            patternIndex = 3;
          } catch {
            diffusionBlocked = true;
          }
        }
      }
      if (patternIndex === 1 && noiseTexture.width !== 64) {
        noiseTexture.image = getBlueNoise();
        noiseTexture.width = 64;
        noiseTexture.height = 64;
        noiseTexture.needsUpdate = true;
      }

      const texelsPerPixel = image ? (viewUniforms.uCover.value[0] * image.naturalWidth) / canvas.width : 1;
      viewUniforms.tMask.value = masks[0].texture;
      viewUniforms.uCell.value = cell;
      viewUniforms.uLod.value = Math.log2(Math.max(cell * texelsPerPixel, 1));
      viewUniforms.uPattern.value = patternIndex;
      viewUniforms.uPalette.value = s.palette === 'rgb' ? 1 : 0;
      viewUniforms.uLevels.value = Math.max(2, Math.round(s.levels));
      viewUniforms.uInk.value = hexToRgb(s.inkColor);
      viewUniforms.uPaper.value = hexToRgb(s.paperColor);
      viewUniforms.uRimColor.value = hexToRgb(s.rimColor);
      viewUniforms.uRim.value = Math.min(Math.max(s.rim, 0), 0.95);
      viewUniforms.uContrast.value = s.contrast;
      viewUniforms.uBrightness.value = s.brightness;
      viewUniforms.uReverse.value = s.reverse ? 1 : 0;
      const intro = image ? Math.min(1, (now - introStart) / INTRO_MS) : 0;
      viewUniforms.uIntro.value = 1 - Math.pow(1 - intro, 2);
      renderer.render({ scene: viewMesh });

      const busy =
        pointer.inside || wanderOn || presence > 0.002 || bursts.length > 0 || now < trailUntil || (image && intro < 1);
      if (busy && visible) raf = requestAnimationFrame(frame);
    };

    const wake = () => {
      if (raf || !visible) return;
      last = performance.now();
      raf = requestAnimationFrame(frame);
    };
    wakeRef.current = wake;

    const img = new Image();
    img.crossOrigin = 'anonymous';
    img.decoding = 'async';
    img.onload = () => {
      image = img;
      imageTexture.image = img;
      try {
        if (samplerContext) {
          const edge = measureEdge(samplerContext, img);
          viewUniforms.uMatte.value = edge.matte;
          viewUniforms.uKey.value = edge.plain ? 1 : 0;
        }
      } catch {}
      introStart = performance.now();
      wake();
    };
    img.src = src;

    const locate = e => {
      const rect = container.getBoundingClientRect();
      pointer.x = e.clientX - rect.left;
      pointer.y = e.clientY - rect.top;
      pointer.placed = true;
    };
    const onMove = e => {
      locate(e);
      if (!pointer.inside) {
        pointer.inside = true;
        pointer.fresh = true;
      }
      wake();
    };
    const onLeave = () => {
      pointer.inside = false;
      wake();
    };
    const onDown = e => {
      onMove(e);
      if (!settingsRef.current?.clickBurst || (e.pointerType === 'mouse' && e.button !== 0)) return;
      bursts.push({ x: pointer.x, y: pointer.y, start: performance.now() });
      if (bursts.length > MAX_BURSTS) bursts.shift();
    };
    container.addEventListener('pointermove', onMove, { passive: true });
    container.addEventListener('pointerenter', onMove, { passive: true });
    container.addEventListener('pointerdown', onDown, { passive: true });
    container.addEventListener('pointerleave', onLeave, { passive: true });
    container.addEventListener('pointercancel', onLeave, { passive: true });

    const resizeObserver = new ResizeObserver(() => {
      layout();
      wake();
    });
    resizeObserver.observe(container);
    const intersectionObserver = new IntersectionObserver(([entry]) => {
      visible = entry.isIntersecting;
      wake();
    });
    intersectionObserver.observe(container);

    layout();
    wake();

    return () => {
      cancelAnimationFrame(raf);
      wakeRef.current = () => {};
      resizeObserver.disconnect();
      intersectionObserver.disconnect();
      img.onload = null;
      container.removeEventListener('pointermove', onMove);
      container.removeEventListener('pointerenter', onMove);
      container.removeEventListener('pointerdown', onDown);
      container.removeEventListener('pointerleave', onLeave);
      container.removeEventListener('pointercancel', onLeave);
      gl.getExtension('WEBGL_lose_context')?.loseContext();
      if (canvas.parentNode) canvas.parentNode.removeChild(canvas);
    };
  }, [src]);

  return <div ref={containerRef} className={`dither-veil ${className}`.trim()} style={style} />;
};

export default DitherVeil;
```
